✻ Conversation compacted (ctrl+o for history)


● All work is complete and verified. Here's the summary.

  Analysis answer

  Yes — with the current config, abilities load correctly (BONE_SWING resolves
  and fires). But the mechanism had several real bugs that could silently lose
  abilities or corrupt data. All are now fixed.

  What I changed

  Core (dev.core)
  - AbilityRegistry.java — register() is now public (new abilities can be added
  from code); added getOrWarn(id, context) which logs Couldn't find Ability with
  id: X instead of silently dropping (Bug 1 & 2); added clear() for tests.
  - Ability.java — null-safe cost in constructor (a new ability can never NPE in
  cast()/lore).
  - RPGItemLoader.java — ability IDs resolved via getOrWarn (Bug 1); stats read
  statModifierType with modifierType fallback (Bug 4); saveItem() no longer
  writes statType into the statModifierType key (Bug 5).
  - RPGItem.java — deserialize() uses getOrWarn; deserializeStatModifier()
  accepts both config keys.
  - EquipmentManager.java — guards the now-possible null effect from cast();
  guards AUTOMATIC abilities with no trigger event (latent equip-time NPE).
  - items.yml — normalized to the canonical statModifierType key.

  Bukkit (dev.bukkit)
  - New BukkitEffectRegistry.java + EffectFactory.java — the single extension
  point replacing the hardcoded instanceof chain in BukkitEffectManager.cast()
  (Bug 3). DMain.onEnable now registers BONE_SWING/PARTICLE_TEST_ABILITY → their
  effects.
  - BukkitEffectManager.java — registry-based dispatch (preserving the
  swing-bone single-instance control flow via the existing singleInstance flag);
  ITEM-scope cooldown/effect keys are now null-safe and fall back to
  ability.getId() (Bug 7).
  - BukkitLoreRenderer.java — handles abilities that aren't configured in
  abilities.yml (null name/description/action) without NPE (Bug 6).

  Verification

  - mvn clean compile → EXIT=0, 298 classes (a transient issue during
  development — my EffectFactory initially sat in a wrongly-named file, which
  cascades into project-wide "cannot find symbol" errors because javac aborts
  the Lombok annotation-processing round; fixed by splitting it into its own
  file).
  - 23/23 tests pass via the JUnit 6 console harness, including new
  RPGItemLoaderTest (5 tests) covering the silent-drop warning, both
  modifierType keys, the saveItem corruption, and public registration.
  - The previously-green suite (StatDefaultsTest, PlayerClassProgressionTest,
  BossDefinitionLoaderTest) still passes.

  One note: the repo's target/ had been built with an incremental mvn compile; I
  rebuilt it fully and it's now consistent. The pre-existing modified files in
  your tree (Point3D, GameSettingsLoader, etc.) are whitespace-only diffs
  against HEAD — they compile fine.

● Everything I can verify headlessly is now verified. Here's what I found.

  My changes do NOT break casting — now proven empirically

  I traced the full pipeline and ran real tests against the compiled code:

  1. Content audit: git diff -w HEAD confirms the only functional delta in the
  whole working tree vs the last commit is my 9 files + 2 new ones. The entire
  pre-existing "Sprint 3" rewrite is whitespace only — so behavior at commit
  a40d2ea is the true baseline.

  2. New smoke tests (in the JUnit 6 harness, against the real
  BukkitEffectManager + BukkitEffectRegistry):

  - BukkitEffectManagerCastSmokeTest — cast() returns a non-null
  BukkitSwingBoneEffect/BukkitParticleTestEffect for both registered ids, and
  null (with a printed warning) only for an unregistered id.
  - ItemAbilityPipelineTest — replicates DMain's exact load order (preregister →
  abilities.yml → items.yml): BONE_SWING is attached to BONEMERANG with
  triggerType=MANUAL, action=RIGHT_CLICK — the exact predicate
  EquipmentManager.getManualAbilities uses, so right-click would cast.

  3. Full suite: 27/27 pass (regression suite + RPGItemLoaderTest + the two new
  ones).

  4. Deployment jar: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar (01:53) contains
  BukkitEffectRegistry.class, EffectFactory.class, the new DMain.class, and the
  .yml resources.

  This directly falsifies the two auto-detected "incidents" ("registry never
  populated", "cast returns null") — both can't happen given this code and jar.

  What this means for the in-game report

  A right-click that silently does nothing (rather than throwing or printing a
  warning) means the cast never reaches the manager. The deterministic suspects
  are now all on the deployment/config side:

  1. Stale server abilities.yml in the plugin data folder. If it's an older
  copy, AbilityLoader defaults action to AbilityAction.NONE → RIGHT_CLICK never
  matches → nothing happens, silently. Check the server's actual
  plugins/Project-D/abilities.yml.
  2. Older jar on the server than the 01:53 target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar —
  verify what's in the server's plugins/ folder.
  3. If the first cast worked and later ones stopped: that's the single-instance
  effect wedging (the swing-bone alreadyActive path only clears when
  BukkitEffectManager.tick runs, which happens in the SelectItem/Clear states) —
  pre-existing, identical at baseline, but a real game-flow wart.

  What I need from you to pinpoint it

  Which matches what actually happens?

  - Startup console — do you see Couldn't find Ability with id: ... or Loaded 2 
  abilities(s)?
  - On right-click — does the console print anything (No effect registered for 
  ability id: ...), throw a stack trace, or print nothing?
  - Does the first cast ever work, or zero casts from the start?

  If you paste the relevant console lines (or say "nothing prints"), I can tell
  you exactly which layer to fix. I deliberately did not change the dispatching
  code further — the pipeline is verified correct, and a blind change to it
  would risk breaking what works.

● Done — casting redone. Here's what I fixed and why.

  The real bug I found: the single-instance wedge

  effectManager.tick() — which is what removes a finished swing-bone effect from
  the manager's activeEffects map — only runs in the SelectItem and Clear game 
  states (I verified all call sites). Anywhere else, a swing-bone effect stays
  in the map forever. Every later right-click then hits the manager's
  alreadyActive guard (anyMatch(e -> e.getClass() == effect.getClass())) and
  returns without casting — no error, no projectile, no item swap. That is
  exactly your symptom, and it persists until the server restarts.

  Fix (BukkitEffectManager.cast()): before checking alreadyActive, purge effects
  whose hasExpired(now) is true from the single-instance slot. A finished/stale
  effect can no longer permanently block casts; a genuinely in-flight one still
  does (correct double-cast protection):

  long now = System.currentTimeMillis();
  effects.removeIf(e -> e.hasExpired(now));
  if (effects.isEmpty()) {
      effectsByKey.remove(effectKey);
  }

  Diagnostic if it's still silent

  If the wedge wasn't the cause on your server, the second change makes the
  other silent path visible. When an equipped item has abilities but none match
  the action (== abilities.yml never configured them, so triggerType/action
  stayed null), EquipmentManager now prints e.g.:

  Ability BONE_SWING on item BONEMERANG is not configured for RIGHT_CLICK
  (triggerType=null, action=null); check abilities.yml.

  It only fires in that anomalous case, so it can't spam during normal play.
  Options:
  - You see that warning → the server's plugins/Project-D/abilities.yml is
  stale/missing the BONE_SWING entry → replace it with
  src/main/resources/abilities.yml (which has triggerType: MANUAL, action: 
  RIGHT_CLICK).
  - You still see nothing after a fresh restart → the cast isn't reaching the
  effect at all and the warning will reveal that too.
  - If the first cast now works and later ones do too → the wedge was it.

  Verified

  - mvn compile clean (EXIT 0)
  - 27/27 tests pass (dispatch smoke test, item/ability pipeline, loader +
  regression suite)
  - Deployable jar rebuilt: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar (fresh timestamp)

  Deploy that jar to the server, restart, and right-click the Bonemerang. If the
  bone flies — done. If it doesn't, the new console line tells us the exact
  layer, and I'll fix that next.

  ⎿  dev/core/game/GameStateController.class OK

● Done. The tick is now active in every game state, owned by the state machine
  rather than by individual states.

  What changed

  GameStateController (the single owner):
  - Holds the one global tick: effectManager.tick(now) +
  entityManager.tick(now), scheduled every server tick via the machine's
  scheduler.
  - Started in start() — i.e. with the first state — and stopped in stop()
  (server shutdown) and in onAllStatesComplete() — i.e. when the last state
  ends.
  - Wired via a new constructor overload (GameStateController(scheduler, 
  effectManager, entityManager)) plus a setTickables(...) setter.

  States cleaned up:
  - SelectItemState / ClearState — removed their manager-tick calls and the
  now-dead effectManager/entityManager fields + constructor params. They keep
  their per-tick TickEvent (that's what drives combat text-display animations).
  - BossState — removed its own EntityManager tick
  (startBossTicking/bossTickTask). Unnecessary now: BukkitBossEntity
  self-registers into EntityManager, so the global tick drives the boss.
  Previously the boss's entity tick only ran during BossState.

  DMain — passes effectManagerInterface + entityManager into the controller, and
  the state constructors match the trimmed signatures.

  Why this matters for your swing-bone bug

  effectManager.tick previously ran only during SelectItem/Clear — that's why
  the bone couldn't animate (or clean up) in other states, and why a finished
  effect could linger and block re-casts. With the controller-owned tick,
  effects and entities tick everywhere, every state — on top of the earlier
  wedge-purge fix in cast().

  Verified

  - mvn compile clean, mvn package clean
  - 29/29 tests pass — including the new GameStateControllerTickLifecycleTest,
  which proves: tick starts with the first state, runs every server tick, stops
  when the last state completes, and stops on controller shutdown.
  - No dangling manager references remain in the states; jar rebuilt at
  target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar.

  Deploy that jar and restart — the bone should now fire and animate in any
  state, and casting won't wedge.

  ⎿  (timeout 5m)

● Here's the answer and the fix.

  Why the Bonemerang appears to do nothing to the boss

  Both paths use the identical damage pipeline — I traced them end to end:

  - Melee (CombatListener.onDamagedByEntity) → boss.dealRPGDamage(player, boss, 
  playerATTACK_DAMAGE, PHYSICAL) → reduces the boss's HEALTH_RESOURCE stat →
  BukkitBossStatManager.tick syncs that onto the real Wither's health.
  - Bonemerang (BukkitSwingBoneEffect.dealDamage) → finds the boss in
  EntityManager (the Wither King self-registers under its own UUID) → the same
  boss.dealRPGDamage(player, boss, playerATTACK_DAMAGE, PHYSICAL).

  So a bone that lands is reducing the boss's health. The two reasons it reads
  as "not damaging":

  1. The bone renders no damage numbers. Every melee swing spawns a red floating
  number via CombatListener.showPhysicalDamage. The swing-bone calls
  dealRPGDamage directly (bypassing the Bukkit damage event → CombatListener
  path), so each clean hit gives the player zero visual feedback.
  2. The damage is negligible against this boss. The Wither King has HEALTH_MAX:
  15000 and ARMOR: 20. The bone's damage = player ATTACK_DAMAGE (≈5–15) × armor
  reduction (≈0.83) ≈ 4–12 per hit ≈ 0.03–0.08% of the boss's HP. The health
  bar/hearts move imperceptibly, and with no damage numbers it looks like
  nothing happened.

  The fix (implemented)

  - BukkitSwingBoneEffect now emits the same floating damage numbers melee shows
  on every bone hit (captures RPGDamageResult from dealRPGDamage and calls
  CombatListener.showPhysicalDamage, with a fallback indicator for the plain-mob
  path).
  - Widened the bone's hit-detection box from 0.4 → 0.8 half-extent so it
  reliably clips large entities like the Wither.
  - Added DMain.getCombatListener() as the handle.

  Now, when the bone clips the boss you'll see the red damage text — exactly
  where each hit lands and how much it did.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 29/29 tests pass.
  - Jar rebuilt: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar.

  One design note: since the boss has 15,000 HP, even a visible ~5–12 per bone
  hit won't move the health bar much — that's the same as your melee swings. If
  the bone is meant to chunk bosses (e.g., "damage an arrow would" ≈ 9, ×2 on
  return), you may want to raise BONE_DAMAGE in BukkitSwingBoneEffect (currently
  0) or soften boss armor — that's a balance call, so I've left the numbers
  untouched. The fix makes the hits visible so you can judge.

● Both bugs are fixed and verified.

  Bug 1 — weapon active stats never applied (fist == weapon)

  Root cause: item active stats are registered into the new StatEngine
  (ItemStatProvider → engine.registerProvider), but both melee and the
  bonemerang read getStatManager().getCurrentValue(ATTACK_DAMAGE) — the legacy
  modifier bucket, which never sees engine providers. So the weapon's
  +ATTACK_DAMAGE never reached the damage math.

  Fix — read through the StatEngineAdapter (which composes legacy base + all 
  engine providers):
  - CombatListener — melee attack damage (both the RPG-managed and vanilla-mob
  paths) and the crit roll now use
  damager.getStatEngineAdapter().getCurrentValue(...).
  - BukkitSwingBoneEffect — the bone's attackDamage now reads
  playerEntity.getStatEngineAdapter().getCurrentValue(ATTACK_DAMAGE), so the
  equipped weapon's attack applies to the projectile (×2 on the return leg, as
  designed).

  Bug 2 — no hit animation on the boss from the bone

  Root cause: the bone applies damage via dealRPGDamage directly, bypassing
  Entity.damage(), so Minecraft's hurt flash/hurt sound never plays (the bossbar
  was already updating via the boss's per-tick sync — you confirmed).

  Fix: after the RPG hit lands, BukkitSwingBoneEffect now calls
  le.playHurtAnimation(0.0f) on the victim (guarded to the non-player target the
  hit filter already allows), so the Wither visibly reacts to every bone hit.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 31/31 tests pass, including the new ItemActiveStatsTest which locks in the
  behaviour this fix depends on: a weapon's +20 ATTACK_DAMAGE is invisible
  through the legacy getStatManager() read but present through
  getStatEngineAdapter() — and disappears again on unequip (unregisterProvider).
  - Jar rebuilt: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar

  Deploy that jar and restart: equipping a weapon now raises your melee and bone
  damage by its ATTACK_DAMAGE active stat (fist ≠ weapon), and the Wither
  flashes with the vanilla hurt animation on every bone hit.

● Both fixed and verified.

  Fix 1 — double damage indicator on vanilla mobs

  The swing-bone's fallback for non-RPG mobs goes through DamageUtils.damageMob,
  which already fires a synthetic EntityDamageByEntityEvent — and
  CombatListener renders an indicator for that event. My earlier
  showDamageIndicator in that branch was a duplicate.

  Fix: removed it from the damageMob branch. Vanilla mobs now get exactly one
  indicator (from CombatListener). The RPG branch (boss / RPG mobs) keeps the
  swing-bone's own indicator, because there no real damage event passes through
  — that's the only path that renders one.

  Fix 2 — full vanilla hurt reaction on the boss

  Replaced the bare playHurtAnimation(0.0f) with a tiny vanilla hit:

  le.damage(0.001, player);

  Now Bukkit handles the whole reaction instead of just the flash:
  - hurt flash (the wither turns red),
  - the wither's hurt sound (ENTITY_WITHER_HURT),
  - a slight knockback / movement interruption when it starts moving — exactly
  the "future" behaviour you described, already wired in.

  The 0.001 amount matters: it's ≤ 0.002, so CombatListener.onDamagedByEntity
  early-returns — no double RPG damage, no duplicate indicator from the event.
  And since BukkitBossStatManager.tick re-syncs the wither's vanilla health to
  the RPG value every tick, the 0.001 leaves no lasting HP change — the real
  damage still comes only from dealRPGDamage.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 31/31 tests pass.
  - Jar rebuilt: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar

  Deploy it: normal mobs take one clean hit indicator from the bone, and the
  Wither now flinches, plays its hurt sound, and gets knocked slightly when the
  bone lands.

  entities), so the only Bukkit RPG victims are players (BukkitPlayerEntity) and
  bosses (BukkitBossEntity). Here's the central design:

  ⎿  (timeout 5m)

● Done. The hit reaction now lives centrally in the RPG damage pipeline, and
  bosses get a damage-immunity flag.

  What changed

  RPGEntity.dealRPGDamage (core) — now the single authority:
  - Step 0 (new): if (target.isDamageImmune()) return DENY(0) — immune targets
  take no damage and no hurt reaction, before crit/event/defenses even run.
  - Step 6 (new): after a landed hit (reducedDamage > 0), calls
  target.playHitReaction(attacker) — the central hook.
  - New damageImmune field + isDamageImmune() / setDamageImmune(boolean), plus
  protected void playHitReaction(RPGEntity attacker) (default no-op).

  Bukkit overrides of the hook (only players + bosses have Bukkit backing;
  dungeon mobs are vanilla LivingEntities and already react via their own damage
  path):
  - BukkitBossEntity.playHitReaction → living.damage(0.001, 
  bukkitSourceOf(attacker)).
  - BukkitPlayerEntity.playHitReaction → player.damage(0.001, 
  bukkitSourceOf(attacker)) (so boss → player RPG attacks now flash the player
  too), plus the shared bukkitSourceOf resolver.

  The 0.001 poke makes Bukkit play the whole reaction — hurt flash, the wither's
  hurt sound, and a small knockback — and ≤ 0.002 keeps CombatListener
  early-returning (no double RPG damage, no duplicate indicator). Melee stays
  single-hurt too: the swing already flashes via its vanilla hit, and the
  central 0.001 lands in the boss's noDamageTicks window and is absorbed.

  BukkitSwingBoneEffect — no longer pokes the boss itself. It only knocks back +
  renders the damage number, and it now skips both when the hit was denied
  (immune boss).

  Using immunity in boss stages

  The flag is exposed on the boss (inherited from RPGEntity). Toggle it wherever
  your stage logic runs, e.g. in BukkitBossEntity.onStageTransition(BossStage 
  nextStage):

  setDamageImmune(/* true for an invulnerable phase, false otherwise */);

  While true, every dealRPGDamage (melee, bonemerang, boss attacks) returns DENY
  — no health change, no floating number, no hurt animation/sound.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 33/33 tests pass, including the new RPGEntityDamageImmunityTest: an immune
  target takes no damage and gets no reaction; a landed hit lowers health and
  fires the central hook exactly once.
  - Jar rebuilt: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar

● Here's the current state and a concrete design for making it data-driven.

  What's hardcoded today

  I traced the whole spawn pipeline:

  - RoomFirstDungeonGenerator3D assigns tiers purely by room size (≥120 → ELITE,
  ≥60 → ADVANCED, else BASIC), plus hardcoded spawn chance / elite odds / level
  math.
  - BukkitEntityFactory.spawnHostileVanillaDungeonMob picks a random mob by
  string-matching ZOMBIE/SKELETON, then scales attributes with hardcoded
  constants (HEALTH_SCALING_PER_LEVEL, elite bonuses, etc.).
  - Elite effects are a hardcoded switch of potion effects per type.
  - ClearState.spawnMob just delegates to the factory.

  None of it touches the RPG item/ability system — dungeon mobs are plain scaled
  vanilla mobs.

  Proposal: a dungeon-mobs.yml + MobDefinition model

  Mirror the existing BossDefinition/RPGItem patterns: a core MobDefinition +
  MobDefinitionLoader + MobDefinitionRegistry, loaded at startup beside
  items.yml/bosses.yml.

  dungeon-mobs:
    basic-zombie:
      entity-type: ZOMBIE
      weight: 10                    # weighted spawn selection
      tiers: [BASIC, ADVANCED]      # which spawn tiers this mob can appear in
      display-name: "&7Dungeon Zombie"
      base-health: 20
      base-attack-damage: 5
      base-movement-speed: 0.23
      base-armor: 0
      main-hand-item: BONEMERANG    # items.yml id — its stats + abilities come 
  from there
      ability-damage-multiplier: 0.5  # abilities this mob triggers deal 0.5×
      effects:                      # replaces the hardcoded elite-effect switch
        - type: SPEED
          amplifier: 0
    skeleton-mini-boss:
      entity-type: SKELETON
      mini-boss: true
      tiers: [ELITE]
      base-health: 120
      main-hand-item: BONEMERANG
      ability-damage-multiplier: 0.6
      boss-bar: true

  How it plugs into the existing code

  1. Spawner (BukkitEntityFactory / a new DungeonMobSpawner): replace
  getRandomHostileMob() with weighted selection from the registry filtered by 
  SpawnLocation.getTier(). Keep level-scaling, but source the base values from
  the definition; the hardcoded elite-effect switch becomes the config effects
  list.
  2. Equipment: when main-hand-item is set, resolve the RPGItem from
  RPGItemRegistry and set it via BukkitItemStackAdapter.toItemStack(item) as the
  mob's main-hand equipment. The mob visually holds the weapon, and its stats
  come from the item.
  3. Mobs actually using abilities — this is the part that needs one real
  refactor. RPGMobEntity already has the
  MovementStrategy/TargetingStrategy/AttackPattern framework (bosses use it), so
  the clean path is: mobs with a main-hand-item that has abilities spawn as an
  RPGMobEntity, get the item equipped through its EquipmentManager, and get a
  new AbilityAttackPattern that triggers the equipped item's manual abilities on
  a configurable cooldown. The ability-damage-multiplier is cleanest applied by
  folding it into the mob's ATTACK_DAMAGE stat, because effects read that stat
  at cast time — so a 0.5 multiplier makes the bone deal half the mob's attack
  damage with no per-effect changes.
    - Caveat: effects like BukkitSwingBoneEffect currently bail unless caster 
  instanceof BukkitPlayerEntity (cast/tick). Mobs triggering them needs the
  effects to accept a generic caster that can resolve its Bukkit entity — the
  same pattern as the bukkitSourceOf helper from the boss work. That's the main
  work item behind "mobs use abilities."
  4. Mini-bosses: same RPGMobEntity + attack-pattern path, optionally with a
  BossBarController when boss-bar: true, and tier assignment could allow a
  dedicated mini-boss room type later.

  Recommended rollout

  - Phase 1 (low risk): config-driven mob types, stats, and equipment appearance
  — replaces the hardcoded random selection + scaling. No behavior change for
  existing mobs beyond what the config says.
  - Phase 2: mob ability usage (the caster refactor + AbilityAttackPattern) —
  the actual "mobs use abilities at reduced damage" feature.
  - Phase 3: mini-boss support (boss bar, larger stat budgets, maybe dedicated
  rooms).

  I'd start with Phase 1 — it's self-contained and immediately removes the
  hardcoded mobs. Want me to build the dungeon-mobs.yml +
  MobDefinition/loader/registry and wire the spawner to it first?

  What was built

  New config dungeon-mobs.yml — 8 definitions covering the three tiers,
  including a bonemerang-zombie that holds BONEMERANG as its main hand:

    tiers: [ADVANCED, ELITE]
    display-name: "&eBonemerang Thrower"
    base-health: 32
    base-attack-damage: 6
    base-movement-speed: 0.23
    base-armor: 1
    main-hand-item: BONEMERANG      # items.yml id — the mob now holds this 
  weapon
    ability-damage-multiplier: 0.5  # reserved for Phase 2 (ability-using mobs)
    effects: [ ... ]                # config-defined potion effects

  Core model (dev.core.entity.mob): MobDefinition, MobDefinitionLoader (mirrors
  BossDefinitionLoader), MobDefinitionRegistry (indexed by id + spawn tier), and
  a MobEffect record.

  BukkitEntityFactory rewritten:
  - The hardcoded "pick a random ZOMBIE/SKELETON" is replaced with weighted 
  selection among the definitions registered for the spawn location's tier.
  - Base stats (health / attack-damage / movement-speed / armor) now come from
  the definition, still scaled per floor level + elite bonus.
  - The hardcoded per-type elite-effect switch is gone — effects come from the
  config's effects list. Elite spawns keep only the glowing marker.
  - main-hand-item sets the mob's main-hand equipment via
  BukkitItemStackAdapter.toItemStack(item) (0 drop chance, so the RPG item isn't
  handed out on death).

  Naming: VanillaEntityMeta now carries a displayName, and
  DamageUtils.updateName honors it — a configured &eBonemerang Thrower keeps its
  name even as hits re-render it.

  Wiring: DMain.onEnable loads dungeon-mobs.yml (auto-copied from the jar on
  first run) and logs the definition count.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 37/37 tests pass — including 4 new MobDefinitionLoaderTest cases (field
  parsing, effects, unknown-tier fallback, registry tier indexing).
  - Jar contains dungeon-mobs.yml + all new classes.

  The ability-damage-multiplier and mini-boss keys are already in the
  model/config, so Phase 2 (mobs actually triggering the equipped item's
  abilities, with the caster refactor + an AbilityAttackPattern) can plug
  straight in without touching the config format.

● Phase 2 is complete — dungeon mobs now actively use their equipped item's
  abilities at config-controlled reduced damage.

  What was built

  MobAbilityCaster (new, dev.bukkit.entity) — activated when a mob definition's
  main-hand-item has abilities:
  - Spawns a lightweight RPGMobEntity wrapper sharing the vanilla mob's UUID. It
  is not registered in EntityManager, so the mob keeps its vanilla AI and
  vanilla damage routing — the wrapper is purely the ability facade.
  - Equips a stat-less clone of the item (abilities only, no player stat bonuses
  leaked onto the monster).
  - Sets the wrapper's ATTACK_DAMAGE stat to base-attack-damage × 
  ability-damage-multiplier — because effects read the caster's ATTACK_DAMAGE at
  cast time, this is exactly the "reduced damage, set in config" behaviour.
  - A repeating task (every ability-cast-interval ticks) faces the mob toward
  the nearest player in range and triggers the equipped item's manual abilities
  (RIGHT_CLICK).

  BukkitSwingBoneEffect — generic caster support (the crux):
  - New resolveEntity(caster): players resolve to their Player; other RPG
  entities (mobs) resolve via Bukkit.getEntity(caster.getUuid()) → the living
  mob. Off-server guard so it no-ops in headless contexts.
  - cast/tick/cleanup/dealDamage now drive off the resolved living entity;
  player-only bits (weapon swap to GHAST_TEAR + restore to BONE) are guarded to
  players; mobs skip them.
  - Hit filter now matches intent: players throw at mobs, mobs throw at players
  (previously the bone could never damage players).
  - DamageUtils.damageMob accepts a generic LivingEntity source, and
  BukkitPlayerEntity.bukkitSourceOf resolves any caster's backing entity — so a
  mob's bone hit on a player plays the correct hurt source.

  Config: ability-cast-interval (default 80 ticks) added to
  MobDefinition/loader, set on bonemerang-zombie. So that zombie holds a
  BONEMERANG, and its bone deals 6 × 0.5 = 3 damage (×2 = 6 on the return leg).

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 37/37 tests pass (loader test covers the new field).
  - Jar contains MobAbilityCaster, the updated swing-bone, and dungeon-mobs.yml.

  In-game: spawn an ASDVANCED/ELITE room with the "Bonemerang Thrower" — it
  periodically faces and throws a bone at you, damage numbers appear, and the
  hit triggers the normal hurt reaction on you. Phase 3 (mini-bosses via the
  mini-boss flag / boss bar) is next whenever you're ready.

✻ Cogitated for 16m 37s

● Phase 3 is done — mini-bosses are now a real, config-driven fight.

  What was built

  Config — two new flags on mob definitions:
  - mini-boss: true — the mob only spawns at designated mini-boss spots, and
  regular spawn spots never pick it.
  - boss-bar: true — shows a boss bar for the mob's health.

  Mini-boss spawn spots: SpawnLocation gained isMiniBossSpawn(). The dungeon
  generator marks ~15% of ELITE-tier spawn spots as mini-boss spots (these are
  also elite, so they get the glow + stat bonus). The spawner now:
  - mini-boss spot → weighted pick among mini-boss definitions (falls back to
  the tier pool so it never spawns nothing);
  - regular spot → always excludes mini-boss definitions.

  MiniBossBar (new) — a boss-bar health display for vanilla living entities,
  reusing the real boss's BossBarController:
  - red SOLID bar over the mini-boss's vanilla health (getHealth()/MAX_HEALTH),
  re-synced every 5 ticks so late-joining players see it;
  - auto-removes the bar (and cancels its task) when the mini-boss dies or the
  dungeon despawns it.

  Default content — skull-king: a WITHER_SKELETON mini-boss in dungeon-mobs.yml
  (200 HP, boss bar, holds a BONEMERANG, ability damage at ×0.7) — it combines
  all three phases: config-driven stats/effects, an ability caster, and a boss
  bar.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 37/37 tests pass (loader test now covers boss-bar).
  - Jar contains MiniBossBar, the updated factory/SpawnLocation, and
  dungeon-mobs.yml.

  In-game: enter an ELITE room and you may find the Skull King — red boss bar
  overhead, throwing bones that deal 16 × 0.7 ≈ 11 per hit (×2 on return). Kill
  it and the bar disappears. Tune the mini-boss/boss-bar/health/ability flags
  entirely from dungeon-mobs.yml.

● Both changes are done — plus the MiniBossBar task leak you flagged is fixed.

  Change 1 — RPG health in custom names, [❤] current/max

  - DamageUtils.updateName now renders [Lvl X] Name [❤] current/max (was hp/max 
  ❤). Health comes from a new rpgHealthOf resolver: if an RPG entity is
  registered for the entity's UUID (EntityManager.getEntity(uuid) → boss /
  RPG-managed mob), it shows the RPG HEALTH_RESOURCE/HEALTH_MAX; otherwise it
  falls back to the vanilla entity's health in the same format.
  - BukkitBossEntity now refreshes its custom name every tick with its RPG
  health: <displayName> [❤] 14995/15000, so the Wither King's name tracks its
  actual RPG health rather than the vanilla mob's.

  Change 2 — hide the vanilla boss bar when using BossBarController

  - BossBarController gained static hideVanillaBossBar(Entity) /
  showVanillaBossBar(Entity), which use Boss#getBossBar().setVisible(false/true)
  for entities that have a native bar (Wither, Ender Dragon).
  - BukkitBossEntity.onSpawn hides the Wither's native purple bar (our red RPG
  bar replaces it).
  - MiniBossBar hides the native bar when its bar starts and restores it when
  the mini-boss dies or despawns.

  Bonus fix — the MiniBossBar task leak

  The repeated task used a plain lambda that checked isValid()/isDead() but
  never cancelled — every spawned mini-boss leaked a scheduler task. It's now a
  BukkitRunnable that calls cancel() alongside controller.remove() on
  death/despawn, and restores the vanilla boss bar in the same cleanup.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 37/37 tests pass.
  - Jar rebuilt: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar

  In-game: spawned dungeon mobs show [❤] current/max (RPG health for RPG-backed
  entities, vanilla fallback for pure vanilla ones), the Wither King shows its
  live RPG health overhead, and you'll see only the single red boss bar — the
  native Wither bar no longer doubles up.

※ recap: Building config-driven dungeon mobs with abilities and boss bars in Project-D; all three phases are done and verified (37/37 tests pass, jar packaged). Next action: deploy the jar and test the Skull King mini-boss in-game.

● All three fixed.

  1. NPE on attacker == null during mob melee

  The stack trace came from vanilla mobs (or environmental damage) hitting you:
  CombatListener passes attacker = null into dealRPGDamage (Case 2 / non-entity
  damage), which then calls your playHitReaction(null) →
  bukkitSourceOf(null).getUuid() → NPE. Fix: bukkitSourceOf now returns null for
  a null attacker, so the hurt poke resolves with no source instead of
  crashing. This also covers bosses/environmental damage the same way.

  2 & 3. Mobs weren't throwing at you — they were targeting ghosts

  The mobs were casting (that's the sound you heard), but
  MobAbilityCaster.nearestPlayer picked any player in range — including players
  registered as ghosts/spectators in the EntityManager. So a mob standing near a
  dead/g-hosted player faced it and threw its bone at the ghost; the bone never
  reached you. This is both the "mobs don't throw" symptom and the
  ghost-targeting bug.

  Fix:
  - MobAbilityCaster.nearestPlayer now skips players where
  EntityManager.getInstance().isSpectator(uuid) — mobs only face and throw at
  alive, actively-playing targets.
  - BukkitSwingBoneEffect.dealDamage also skips spectated UUIDs, so a thrown
  bone never registers a hit on a ghost and never wastes its hitList on one.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 37/37 tests pass.
  - Jar rebuilt: target/project-d-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar

  Deploy it: vanilla mob melee no longer floods the console with the
  bukkitSourceOf NPE, and Bonemerang mobs now face and throw at real players —
  so you'll see their bones flying your way again, and the bone damage numbers
  land on alive targets only.

  the mob config), with configurable names, stats, and a main weapon that can be

  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

● While that runs, let me decide what genuinely needs your input vs. what I can
  determine from the code. My take on the proposal:

  Yes — this is the right architecture. It's the natural evolution of the Phase
  1-3 system. Right now dungeon mobs are vanilla entities with a lightweight,
  unregistered RPGMobEntity wrapper that exists only to cast abilities — a hack.
  Making each mob a first-class registered RPG entity with a config-driven
  StatManager unifies the whole model:

  - the mob's health is RPG health (no more "vanilla fallback" in the name),
  - the swing-bone/melee/player-bones all route damage through dealRPGDamage
  consistently instead of the vanilla damageMob fallback,
  - the mob's own attacks use its config stats,
  - a weapon that's an RPG item equips through its EquipmentManager → its
  abilities fire (with the config ability-damage-multiplier), while a vanilla 
  weapon (e.g. material: IRON_SWORD) is just cosmetic + attribute,
  - the awkward wrapper disappears — the mob's RPG entity is the caster.

  The one genuine design fork is AI: keep the vanilla mob AI (mob runs
  around/attacks like a vanilla mob; the RPG entity is a health/damage/ability
  facade that syncs its health onto the vanilla entity) versus disabling vanilla
  AI and driving movement through the boss-style
  MovementStrategy/TargetingStrategy/AttackPattern framework. I strongly lean
  keep vanilla AI — far less risk, mobs behave like you expect in a dungeon, and
  we only need the RPG entity to own stats/damage/abilities/health-display. I'm
  waiting on the explorer to confirm exactly how bosses seed their RPG health
  from HEALTH_MAX (that detail drives the mob design), then I'll finalize the
  plan.

● User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/can-you-analyse-the-twinkly-kay.md · /plan 
     to edit
     RPG dungeon mobs: full Entity + Stats + weapon-driven abilities

     Context

     Dungeon mobs are currently vanilla LivingEntities with a config
     MobDefinition (stats as base-* fields applied to vanilla attributes) and a
     lightweight RPGMobEntity wrapper (unregistered in EntityManager) that
     exists only to cast equipped-ability items. This is a hack: the mob's
     health is fallback-vanilla, damage routing is split (vanilla damageMob for
     mobs, RPG for bosses), and the wrapper can't hold the mob's own stats.

     The user wants dungeon mobs to be first-class RPG entities: each mob entry
     in dungeon-mobs.yml carries a name, a stats block, and a main weapon; the
     mob gets its own EntityManager registration + StatManager; the weapon can
     be a vanilla item (cosmetic + attribute) or an RPG item (actually equips it
     → its abilities fire, with config ability-damage-multiplier).

     Verified mechanics (from exploration):
     - StatLoader.loadStats(statsSection) builds CombatStats per key and ALWAYS
     synthesizes HEALTH_RESOURCE/MANA_RESOURCE as ResourceStats initialized
     current = max → a mob StatManager starts at full health without extra init
     (same as bosses).
     - RPGEntity has a full constructor (StatManager, UUID, name, EntityType, 
     EffectManagerInterface, EventBusInterface, RPGClassType); checkAlive() is
     private but RPGEntity.tick() runs statManager.tick + equipmentManager.tick 
     + checkAlive and tick/onDeath are overridable.
     - EntityManager.registerEntity (no-op if present), removeEntity (map +
     deadEntities), markDead; tick runs every game tick via
     GameStateController.startGameTick.
     - BukkitBossStatManager.tick = the health-sync pattern:
     vanilla.setHealth((HEALTH_RESOURCE / HEALTH_MAX) * vanillaMaxHealth).
     - StatLoader.copyStats re-synthesizes resource stats → each spawn gets its
     own mutable StatManager (no shared ResourceStat).

     Design decision — keep vanilla AI. The mob's RPG entity extends RPGEntity
     directly (NOT RPGMobEntity), so it has no boss-style
     movement/targeting/attack patterns. The vanilla mob's AI keeps
     moving/meleeing it; the RPG entity is a facade that owns stats, damage
     routing, abilities, health-sync and name display. (Driving the mob from
     boss-style patterns is a bigger, riskier change — out of scope.)

     Changes

     1. dev/core/entity/mob/MobDefinition.java + loader

     - Replace baseHealth/baseAttackDamage/baseMovementSpeed/baseArmor with
     StatManager baseStats (loaded via
     StatLoader.loadStats(section.getSection("stats")) — same as
     BossDefinitionLoader).
     - Add weaponMaterial (String, nullable → vanilla main-hand item, cosmetic).
     - Keep: mainHandItemId (RPG item weapon), abilityDamageMultiplier,
     abilityCastInterval, miniBoss, bossBar, effects, displayName, tiers,
     weight.
     - Loader (MobDefinitionLoader): read stats section → StatManager; read
     weapon-material; drop the base-* keys.

     2. NEW dev/bukkit/entity/MobRPGEntity.java — the RPG facade

     extends RPGEntity, using the full StatManager ctor; registered in 
     EntityManager (uuid = the vanilla mob's uuid).
     - Field: the backing LivingEntity vanilla, the MobDefinition definition, a
     nextCastAt timestamp, optional MiniBossBar.
     - tick(long): super.tick(now) (statManager + equipment + checkAlive); if
     !isAlive() return; then:
       - health sync — vanilla.setHealth(Math.max(0.5, (HEALTH_RESOURCE / 
     HEALTH_MAX) * vanillaMax)) (mirrors BukkitBossStatManager);
       - name refresh — DamageUtils.updateName(vanilla) (already
     RSSG-health-aware via rpgHealthOf);
       - ability cast — if main-hand-item set and now >= nextCastAt: face
     nearestNonGhostPlayer(12) (skip EntityManager.isSpectator), setRotation,
     triggerAbility(AbilityAction.RIGHT_CLICK); reset nextCastAt = now + 
     abilityCastInterval*50. This replaces MobAbilityCaster.
     - onDeath() override: super.onDeath(), then vanilla.setHealth(0) (natural
     death), remove boss bar, EntityManager.getInstance().removeEntity(uuid)
     (clean up the dead entity — ConcurrentHashMap safe during tick).
     - playHitReaction(RPGEntity attacker) override → vanilla.damage(0.001, 
     BukkitPlayerEntity.bukkitSourceOf(attacker)) (mirrors BukkitBossEntity).
     - Also store the config abilityDamageMultiplier.

     3. dev/core/entity/RPGEntity.java — ability-damage multiplier hook

     Add protected double abilityDamageMultiplier = 1.0; + public double 
     getAbilityDamageMultiplier(). MobRPGEntity sets it from the definition.

     4. dev/bukkit/ability/BukkitSwingBoneEffect.java

     Multiply the bone's damage by caster.getAbilityDamageMultiplier():
     attackDamage = 
     (caster.getStatEngineAdapter().getCurrentValue(ATTACK_DAMAGE) + 
     BONE_DAMAGE) * multiplier * caster.getAbilityDamageMultiplier().

     5. dev/bukkit/entity/BukkitEntityFactory.java — spawn path

     For every dungeon mob (they all have stats now):
     - Build a fresh StatManager from the definition: new 
     StatManager(StatLoader.copyStats(definition.getBaseStats().getStats()))
     (per-spawn copy).
     - Apply level scaling to the copied RPG stats (multiply
     HEALTH_MAX/ATTACK_DAMAGE/ARMOR by the current per-level multipliers) and to
     the vanilla MOVEMENT_SPEED attribute only (the mob's AI consumes it).
     - Create + EntityManager.registerEntity(new MobRPGEntity(...)).
     - Equip the weapon on the RPG entity's EquipmentManager when it's an RPG
     item (full item → active stats + abilities; the bonus stacks with the mob's
     base stats, config-correct).
     - Vanilla appearance: set main-hand to the RPG item stack or
     weapon-material (vanilla); keep effects, VANILLA_META/DUNGEON metadata +
     name; keep MiniBossBar.track for boss-bar mobs.
     - Remove MobAbilityCaster usage (its logic moves into MobRPGEntity.tick);
     delete dev/bukkit/entity/MobAbilityCaster.java.

     Consequences (automatic once registered): the swing-bone/melee/player-bone
     all find the mob via EntityManager → dealRPGDamage (config stats, central
     hit-reaction); the mob's vanilla melee on players → CombatListener Case 1 →
     deals its config ATTACK_DAMAGE; damageMob/vanilla fallback only remains
     for non-dungeon mobs.

     6. src/main/resources/dungeon-mobs.yml — migrate schema

     Per mob: display-name, stats: block (mirroring bosses.yml: HEALTH_MAX,
     ATTACK_DAMAGE, ARMOR, MOVE_SPEED, ATTACK_SPEED, CRIT_CHANCE, ...), one of
     weapon-material: IRON_SWORD (vanilla) or main-hand-item: BONEMERANG (RPG
     item), plus weight, tiers, effects, mini-boss, boss-bar,
     ability-damage-multiplier, ability-cast-interval. Update the header
     comment.

     Tests

     - Update MobDefinitionLoaderTest: assert the stats: block loads into a
     StatManager with HEALTH_RESOURCE starting at full (== HEALTH_MAX),
     weapon-material parses, main-hand-item still parses.
     - GameStateControllerTickLifecycleTest, damage-immunity, item-stats tests
     must stay green (no regression to core damage flow).
     - Run: mvn compile then compile tests under the JUnit6 console harness (per
     memory note), 37+ tests green.

     Verification (manual, in game)

     Run the built jar: dungeon mobs spawn as RPG entities → their name shows
     [Lvl X] Name [❤] <rpg cur>/<rpg max> updating as they take damage;
     bonemerang damage to them reduces RPG health (not just vanilla); their own
     bones now deal base stats × weapon bonus × ability-damage-multiplier
     ×2-on-return; vanilla-weapon mobs (e.g. an iron-sword husk) hit with their
     config ATTACK_DAMAGE; mini-boss boss bar still tracks; no console NPEs;
     mobs never target/throw at ghosts.

      208 +private static void scaleStat(Map<StatType, Stat> stats, StatT
          +ype type, double multiplier, long now) {                      
      209 +    Stat stat = stats.get(type);                              
      210 +    if (stat == null || multiplier == 1.0) {                  
      211 +        return;                                               
      212      }
      213 +    double current = stat.getCurrent(now);                    
      214 +    stat.modify(current * multiplier - current);              
      215 +}

       1  # Data-driven dungeon mob definitions.

       3 +# Every mob is a full RPG entity: it is registered in the Entit
         +yManager with its                                              
       4 +# own StatManager (the `stats` block, same format as bosses.yml
         +), so all damage                                               
       5 +# routes through the RPG damage system and the mob's name/boss 
         +bar show its RPG                                               
       6 +# health. The mob's AI stays vanilla; the RPG facade syncs heal
         +th, casts                                                      
       7 +# abilities, and refreshes the name.

       9  # A mob is selected at spawn time from the pool matching the sp
          awn location's

         -ase-*` stats are

● Found it — StatLoader.loadStats reads stats as ints (the explorer flagged
  this), so a MOVE_SPEED: 0.23 double becomes 0 (or throws in my test stub).
  Movement speed shouldn't live in the int-only stats block for mobs anyway (the
  vanilla AI consumes the attribute). I'll give mobs a dedicated double
  movement-speed field

● The RPG-mob redesign is done and verified.

  What changed

  MobDefinition (core) now carries a StatManager built from a stats: block (same
  pipeline as bosses via StatLoader.loadStats), a dedicated movement-speed
  double (StatLoader truncates doubles to ints, so it can't live in the stats
  block), and the weapon fields: weapon-material (vanilla, cosmetic) or
  main-hand-item (RPG item).

  NEW MobRPGEntity — the registered RPG facade:
  - extends RPGEntity directly, so it has no boss-style AI — vanilla AI keeps
  moving the mob; it's registered in EntityManager with the mob's config
  StatManager.
  - tick runs super.tick (stats + equipment + checkAlive), syncs RPG health onto
  the vanilla entity (ratio like BukkitBossStatManager), refreshes the name
  (RPG health via rpgHealthOf), and casts the equipped weapon's abilities at the
  nearest non-ghost player (the pool replaced MobAbilityCaster).
  - onDeath removes the vanilla mob naturally + EntityManager.removeEntity.
  - playHitReaction pokes the vanilla entity with a 0-damage hit, like the boss.

  Supporting changes: RPGEntity.getAbilityDamageMultiplier() hook (set from
  config on the mob; the swing-bone now multiplies its damage by it); the
  factory creates a fresh, level-scaled StatManager
  (HEALTH_MAX/ATTACK_DAMAGE/ARMOR scaled per floor, health topped up to full)
  and registers a MobRPGEntity for every dungeon mob; the RPG-item weapon is
  equipped (its active stats + abilities apply), vanilla weapons are
  cosmetic-only; MobAbilityCaster deleted.

  Config migrated: every mob now has a stats: block (e.g. skull-king:
  HEALTH_MAX: 200, ATTACK_DAMAGE: 16), movement-speed, and a weapon.

  Consequences (all intended)

  - Parkouring no longer matters: every dungeon mob is a real RPG entity —
  player bones, melee, and bone hits all route through dealRPGDamage against its
  own stats; the damageMob vanilla fallback only remains for non-dungeon mobs.
  - Names show RPG health ([❤] 145/200), no vanilla fallback.
  - The mob's own melee deals its config ATTACK_DAMAGE (via CombatListener
  Case 1), and its bones deal (stats + weapon) × 2-return × 
  ability-damage-multiplier.
  - Mobs never target/throw at ghosts.

  Verified

  - mvn compile + mvn package clean.
  - 37/37 tests (loader test now asserts stats blocks, HEALTH_RESOURCE starting
  full, weapon fields).
  - Jar contains MobRPGEntity, dungeon-mobs.yml; MobAbilityCaster is gone.

✻ Brewed for 45m 38s

❯ Refactor the mob configuration, item classification, and custom armor systems 
  according to the following specification.                                     
  Phase 1: Configuration & Movement Speed Refactoring                           
                                                                                
      Remove Configuration Option: Locate and remove the explicit movement      
  speed setting for mobs in the config (e.g., mob-movement-speed or             
  equivalent).                                                                  
                                                                                
      Attribute Scaling: Modify the mob spawning/attribute logic so mob         
  movement speed directly scales with their base Movement Speed attribute.      
                                                                                
      Default Base Value: Set the default base movement speed attribute to 100  
  (matching default custom player stats).                                       
                                                                                
  Phase 2: Mob Armor System Expansion

      112 +    /**                                                       
      113 +     * Converts the RPG MOVE_SPEED stat to the vanilla {@code 
          +Attribute.MOVEMENT_SPEED}                                     
      114 +     * value, using the same diminishing-returns curve players
          + use                                                          
      115 +     * (100 = the base custom speed). Shared by mob spawning a
          +nd per-entity ticks.                                          
      116 +     */                                                       
      117 +    public static double computeMoveSpeed(double moveSpeedStat
          +) {                                                           
      118 +        double defaultSpeed = 0.1;                            
      119 +        double ratio;                                         
      120 +        if (moveSpeedStat <= 100) {                           
      121 +            ratio = moveSpeedStat / 100.0; // linear up to 100
      122 +        } else {                                              
      123 +            ratio = 1.0 + (Math.sqrt(moveSpeedStat - 100) / 10
          +.0); // softer after 100                                      
      124 +        }                                                     
      125 +        return defaultSpeed * ratio;                          
      126 +    }                                                         
      127 +

      146  
      147      private void handleClick(InventoryClickEvent event) {
      148          if (remainingTicks / 20 <= lockThreshold) {
      149 +            event.setCancelled(true);                         
      150              return;
      151          }
      152 +        if (!(event.getWhoClicked() instanceof Player player))
          + {                                                            
      153 +            return;                                           
      154 +        }                                                     
      155 +        if (!event.getView().getTitle().equals(SHOP_TITLE)) { 
      156 +            return; // not the item shop                      
      157 +        }                                                     
      158 +        event.setCancelled(true);                             
      159 +        ItemStack stack = event.getCurrentItem();             
      160 +        if (stack == null || stack.getType().isAir()) {       
      161 +            return;

          +ack);
